ParlaMint-ES_2018-05-23-CD180523.u2	Very good morning. Thank you, President. Ladies and gentlemen, I appear in this House to explain the draft General State Budgets of Section 26, Ministry of Health, Social Services and Equality... [[Rumors]]
ParlaMint-ES_2018-05-23-CD180523.u3	Silence, please, silence.
ParlaMint-ES_2018-05-23-CD180523.u4	A global project designed for people, for all Spanish families, in the preservation of the welfare state and in the future of the next generations. From the People's Party government we are very clear that all Spanish families are the undisputed protagonists in the history of our country. Spanish families are the most important for the Government of Spain and CIS says that 98.7% of Spaniards also have the most important family. Thanks to the efforts of the Spanish families, the reformist policies of the Spanish Government and President Rajoy, we have come together from one of the worst economic crises we remember. These General State Budgets are the most social in the history of democracy because we are a government that we think of and care for every family. As far as the Ministry of Health, Social Services and Equality is concerned, we are working to achieve six major objectives for all Spaniards and all Spanish families: equal opportunities and job creation; family protection with fiscal measures, economic and social aid; reconciliation and co-responsibility measures; attention to the dependence and care of our elderly; the eradication of all forms of violence against women by simply being women; and the care of the health of our families because they are most important to us. We have in the ministry and in this budget more than 2,200 million euros, 1,900 million for social services and equality, 380 million for the General Secretariat of Health and Consumption and the undersecretariat. And if the amendments - which are likely to be approved - are adopted and we add the amounts in this budget, this ministry will have a total of 2399 million euros, which represents an increase over last year of almost 10%. Here I would like to add the 4 170 million euros of the Imserso dedicated to dependence and to the attention of our elders. I'm starting with the health area. Our National Health System is an example of the great heritage that we have built for all Spaniards over the past forty years. A heritage that is counted in years of life - we are the longest in Europe and the second largest in the world - a heritage that translates into the highest standards of quality, cohesion, equity and security and that allows the Spanish model to be a model to follow. Our health system has its own name in the world and this is recognized by the most prestigious international publications and the World Health Organization itself, but also by all Spanish people, since three weeks ago we presented the last health barometer where Spanish patients put us the highest score of the last ten years and put a remarkable mark on the National Health System. They give us 78 % of Spanish satisfaction in Spanish emergencies, 83 % satisfaction in specialist consultations, 86 % satisfaction in hospital income and 87 % satisfaction in primary care centres. Therefore, these data are the result of these last ten years but I would like to recall that in the last seven years there has been a People's Party government that always thinks about the health of people and our families and, together with the autonomous communities since the Interterritorial Council of the National Health System, we can say that we have one of the best national health systems in the world. These budgets pursue, above all, to continue to lead this National Health System, five major objectives. The first axis, the promotion of health as an indispensable recipe for a healthier and healthier society; the second axis, eHealth and digitalization with the aim of transforming our health and positioning Spain in the digital health revolution; the third axis, research, development and innovation, great challenges in our management of the health of the 21st century; the fourth axis, working the confidence of professionals, patients and consumers, vertebrate axes of our National Health System, and the fifth axis, strengthening funding so that our health system has sufficient funding guaranteeing cohesion, quality, equity and security. Therefore, as far as health promotion is concerned, you know that according to the WHO, 23% of deaths occurring annually worldwide are related to environmental causes such as air pollution, excessive alcohol consumption, tobacco, unhealthy diets or sedentary lifestyles. These cause diseases such as stroke, heart disease, respiratory disease, and cancer. I would therefore like to highlight four major strategic actions in this budget to work on prevention and health promotion. First, we are planting overweight and we are doing so with the collaborative plan for improving the composition of food and drink where from this year until 2020 we will reduce 10% of the quantities of sugar, salt and saturated fats of the products in the shopping basket. Secondly, these budgets support this government's goal of combating smoking and we have therefore updated the Tobacco Act that we approved via royal decree-law at the end of 2017. Thirdly, we are committed to combating all kinds of addictions, especially those caused by the irresponsible use of new technologies by our young people. That is why we approved the National Addiction Strategy just two months ago and therefore this budget will allow us to implement it. [[Rumors]] Fourthly, the great measure of prevention is that we are promoting and finalizing the draft law on alcohol-free children. Also with the approval of these budgets we will be able to develop new strategies for disease prevention that are currently the most prevalent in Spain..., [[Rumors]]
ParlaMint-ES_2018-05-23-CD180523.u5	Quiet, please.
ParlaMint-ES_2018-05-23-CD180523.u6	... such as cardiovascular diseases, rare or uncommon pathologies, cancer and the development of a new version of the mental health strategy, as well as the strategy of neurodegenerative diseases highlighting ALS, amyotrophic lateral sclerosis. I would like to take this opportunity to thank the Citizens' Parliamentary Group for the agreement that we have reached thanks to an amendment that will increase the budget that will be allocated to the Spanish Association of Amyotrophic Lateral Sclerosis. As for the second axis in health, eHealth and digitization, within the framework of the Plan for the digital transformation of the Spanish economy promoted by the Ministry of Industry, we are working to make digital advances improve the well-being of citizens, especially their health. In this line, Big Data, 5G, Machine Learning, Artificial Intelligence and Industry 4.0 are some of the tools that will help us digitize our health and improve its functioning for diagnosis. Just as we will continue to work - and so it is foreseen in these budgets - with the interoperability and full implementation that we committed at the beginning of the legislature in health card, electronic prescription and digital clinical history, whose compliance percentage levels we have greatly increased. These budgets also ensure that we advance information transparency and a better understanding of the results of the health system, such as the details of investments made in the system and care centres. Thanks to these budgets will be a real portal of the transparency of the National Health System and, therefore, we will have health in data. With regard to the third axis, research, development and innovation, I would like to highlight in this regard the leading role played by the Carlos III Institute of Health, the main public research body in the field of biomedical R & D+i in Spain, which is functionally dependent on the Ministry of Health and in which each year we invest the funds from the sixth additional provision of the Law on Medicinal Products, which in 2017 amounted to 90 million euros. These 90 million have served, among other issues, to develop strategic action in health, to hire research staff and to provide funding to investigate respiratory, metabolic, digestive or liver diseases, as well as cancer. Within this program we are going to boost this year - this is what is foreseen in these budgets - the plan of personalized medicine and precision. Along with research and development, another very important axis is our portfolio of services, which I want to say is one of the best and most complete in Europe. In recent years we have incorporated more than 260 therapeutic innovations into the portfolio - in this year 2018 we have incorporated thirteen therapeutic innovations -, 25% of which focus on the treatment of cancer. These innovations have also enabled us to treat diseases such as hepatitis C through direct action antivirals aimed at more than 103 000 Spanish patients and we have also developed a new model for rare diseases of high economic impact, such as spinal muscle atrophy, which patients have had at their disposal since last March in the basic portfolio. Rare diseases are a priority for the Government of the People's Party and we have demonstrated this with the first national strategy for their early diagnosis and treatment, with the state registry of rare diseases and with the expansion of the network of centres, services and reference units. I would also like to mention the new incorporations in the basic portfolio in 2018: we have progressively increased the age of funding for hearing aids until we reach the age of twenty-six, which is one of the great demands of deaf people and which a People's Party government will make possible. We have also incorporated in the basic portfolio in March the micropigmentation of the breast areola for all women who suffer in their body the consequences of breast cancer. In 2017, more than 26 000 new cases were diagnosed which, thanks to this measure, will find an effective aesthetic solution, especially social and self-esteem. We have also incorporated in the basic portfolio the sensor systems in the arm for children with diabetes, allowing them to be constantly controlled and avoid punctures to measure the level of glucose. Also, as mandated by the UN, by 2050 there will be 10 million people who will die each year from antibiotic-resistant diseases. These General State Budgets will allow us to approve the new Antibiotic Resistance Plan 2019-2021; I mean we have already reduced the misuse of antibiotics by 6 points. These budgets guarantee us the safety of the use of medicines and the quality of their composition, which is complemented by the Strategy to combat counterfeit medicines. Let me close this axis of research, development and innovation with the National Organization of Transplants, which will have a budget of EUR 4,100 000 to continue all NTA programmes, as well as the National Bone marrow Plan or the National Corneal Plan, to consolidate the global leadership we have exercised for more than two decades and to work to make possible the fifty donors per million inhabitants in 2022, which is the goal we have set ourselves. The fourth axis in health care is the strengthening of professionals, patients and consumers and we have worked to promote patient participation in decision-making bodies through their inclusion in the Health Advisory Council and the signing of the framework agreement last October with patients with the aim of promoting their rights to participation, information and consultation in the National Health System in terms of disease management, health promotion and disease prevention. We have also strengthened the safety of citizens as consumers who we are through measures such as the system of consumer alerts, the transposition of the European directive on the alternative resolution of disputes and items such as those envisaged in the budget to improve the functioning of the consumer arbitration system and the European Consumer Centre. For their part, professionals, who are our main asset in the National Health System, are also protagonists in this 2018 budget. I would like to thank all the health professionals in our National Health System for their vocation and excellence, and for maintaining that excellence at times as complicated as the economic crisis. These budgets return the effort of these great health professionals, who are the backbone of our National Health System. There are therefore two very important measures in this budget: the first, the increase in wages over three years, with an increase of up to 8.79 % for all health professionals and all staff in the National Health System, and the second, the call for more than 129 000 places with the extraordinary public offer of job stabilization, the largest in the history of democracy, which will allow us to raise the replacement rate to 100 % and reduce the temporality to 8 %. We are also working on a real decree to promote the presence and stabilization of doctors and specialties in hard-to-cover areas, such as rural areas of Spain. With these measures in favor of professionals we are responding to each and every one of the demands that have taken us both to the Medical and Nurse Conference that we held last March and April - and that is the first time that it is celebrated - as well as to the questions that professionals have offered us at the Professional Forum, created in 2015 and that we have reactivated in this legislature, favoring consensus among professionals and achieving a historical agreement in the field of nursing indication. As you know, the Ministry of Health, Social Services and Equality also manages the competence of Health in Ceuta and Melilla through Ingesa and, therefore, Ingesa has more than 240 million euros in 2018. This budget will bring about the new hospital for Melilla, which, as you know, has been paralyzed for many years by judicial problems of defects in documents and procedures at the time of the socialist governments. Two weeks ago in the Council of Ministers we approved the investment for the new hospital, which amounts to 91 million euros and a work planning of thirty-six months. Therefore, we can say that in the year 2021 it will be a reality the hospital of Melilla for all Melillenses. The works have already begun and will no longer be paralyzed until the construction of the hospital in Melilla is completed. This budget addresses another of the major concerns of Ceuta and Melilla, due to the complications inherent in the physical distance with the peninsula and therefore its difficult stabilization of the staff, and consolidates eighty places of health personnel, putting us in the ratio of the average of the National Health System. I would also like to speak at this point about the outstanding debt that we had all the Spaniards with those affected by thalidomide. There was an investment agreement between the Citizens' Group and the People's Group to end once and for all with this historic debt that Spain had with those affected by thalidomide, especially those affected from 1950 to 1985. These budgets respond to all those affected by thalidomide, with a budget of EUR 20 million. I end with the fifth Health Point, which, as I said, was funding. These budgets include an increase in funding for the autonomous communities of more than €4,200 million. Therefore, this increase in funding will make it possible to devote it to the improvement and preservation of one of the major pillars of our welfare state, which is health. Moreover, also, following the objectives set out at the Sixth Conference of Presidents, held in the Senate in January 2017, the financing of health is a priority for all and a priority that we continue to lead from the ministry with initiatives, convening in the Interterritorial Council the monographic plenary that will be held in the coming weeks, where we want to continue to advance with the autonomous communities in the economic sufficiency of the National Health System. I now turn to social services and equality, to the Secretariat of State. After protecting the health of people, the best way to protect the well-being of Spanish people and all our families is to ensure that employment opportunities are created for all in our country. Yesterday Minister Fatima Báñez gave a good account of how the work measures of our People's Party government have returned opportunities for all people and our families with job creation, which is the best tool and the best social policy. Let me remind you of three very important figures that reflect the commitment of this Government and the commitment of this ministry to work on equal opportunities and thus achieve greater and better employability. Today more than 8.5 million women are working in Spain, the highest figure in history, today there are more than 1 million young people working, something that has not happened for six years and more than a million contracts have been signed for people with disabilities. Therefore, these figures are not by chance. Behind these figures there is talent and there is also political action. In particular, from the Ministry of Health, Social Services and Equality we carry out five major axes of action. The first is to increase equal opportunities for women and men, and we have therefore developed programmes that promote the employability of women in situations of special vulnerability. We increased the budget of this programme by 29%. [[Rumors]]
ParlaMint-ES_2018-05-23-CD180523.u7	Quiet, please!
ParlaMint-ES_2018-05-23-CD180523.u8	We also develop programmes to promote the elimination of stereotypes that prevent equal opportunities for women and men, and we do so with programmes to help small and medium-sized enterprises implement equality plans, as well as with the implementation of the anonymous curriculum. We have increased the budget for these programmes by 20%. Also with the inclusion in these budgets of two new programs to work the elimination of stereotypes in the STEM professions - science, technology, engineering and mathematics - because we want to eliminate the digital gap that exists in the STEM regarding women. Also with programmes to encourage more women in positions of responsibility and thus break the glass ceiling. Apart from continuing to work on the Promotion Program, we are going to launch a new program called Talentia 360, aimed at avoiding the loss of female talent as we move forward in positions of high responsibility. In order to generate greater opportunities for young people, since the Injuve we have launched in these budgets the initiative of Spain, Capital of young talent, because we want to make the promotion of youth talent a national priority and to ensure that in the next three years there are significant changes in the educational and productive social ecosystem of young people because we are very clear that young people are the main engine of the social and cultural changes in our society and because we want the entrepreneurial DNA of Spanish young people to be another sign of our brand Spain. To build a universally accessible society, we are committed to education and employment as the main tools for people with disabilities and will launch this year the first call of the program for improving the educational conditions of people with disabilities through the sleeping accounts with this program, as you know known as the Reina Letizia Program. This programme will serve, for example, to enable people with disabilities to study an Erasmus, to access university, to training courses outside their environment without disability being an impediment and to have all the help they need to get away from their environment and to be able to study at university, vocational training or an Erasmus. At this point, allow me to thank the Citizens' Parliamentary Group for the amendment to the State Confederation of Deaf Persons so that it can continue to perform its role and reach out to more people. The second axis in social services and equality is that of fiscal measures, economic and social aid to families. In addition to ensuring equal opportunities by promoting employment for all individuals and their families, the People's Party Government continues to support and protect families, especially those in a situation of greater vulnerability. And we do so as we have always done, with fiscal measures and economic and social aid. In the tax reform of the IRPF that we launched in 2015, we already consider families as a priority. Therefore, at that time, we increased the family minimums to 32% so that families with children paid less taxes and incorporated social benefits for families as never before in our country, establishing new deductions of €1,200 for families with dependent and ascending children and for large and single-parent families. To all these measures, in this year's budget we add an increase of EUR 600 per child per year in the deduction of a large family and a new deduction of EUR 1,200 per spouse with disabilities per year. In addition, for those families in situations of greatest difficulty, we set up direct economic and social aid, such as the food purchase programme, to which we have allocated almost EUR 100 million, and the programme to combat child poverty, to which we allocated EUR 60 million, which together with the EUR 40 million of the agreed plan, allows us to devote EUR 100 million to strengthening the social aid received by families. To this set of aid we have added to these budgets a new programme which will have a great impact and which for me is one of the star instruments and measures of this budget, as it is that from the approval of the budgets all Spanish companies in a voluntary and anonymous way will be able to mark the crossing of 0.7 % in the company tax. Now that we are in the middle of an income campaign, I encourage everyone to mark the crossing in favor of 0.7% of the IRPF. Therefore, we will now have another programme where all Spanish companies will be able to devote 0.7 % of the company tax to the social programme in order to get to where the administration sometimes does not arrive. If everyone were to mark the crossroads, we would be talking about an annual programme of €700 million. This is therefore the highest social programme in history, and this has been done by a People's Party Government because we are very clear that we want this aid to continue to reach 7.5 million Spaniards with the IRPF. Therefore, with corporation tax we can go much further. This program helps us to vertebrate the third social sector, which in Spain - one of the leading countries in volunteering - has more than 30 000 entities. The third axis is conciliation and co-responsibility. From the Government of Spain of the People's Party we work to advance more and more towards a society of shared roles and to abandon the society of divided roles or of duplicate roles. Therefore, in these budgets we make the extension of paternity leave to five weeks, we incorporate in the IRPF a new deduction of EUR 1 000 per year for childcare costs, which, in addition to facilitating conciliation, also contributes to breaking the poverty chain. We also provide that public administrations can regulate a retrievable exchange of hours, so that workers can make their working hours more flexible and better care for their children, the elderly and people with disabilities. These measures are not new to the Government of the People's Party, because I remind you that it was also we who launched the monthly aid of 100 euros for working mothers with children from zero to three years. All these aids are not exclusive but complementary to the existing aids. It was also we who modified the breastfeeding permit so that it could be enjoyed not only by women but also by men. It was the Government of the People's Party that extended the right to reduce the number of days for the care of children, increasing it from eight to twelve years of age. It was also a Government of the People's Party that established for the first time the maternity supplement in the retirement pensions of working mothers. I'm moving on to the fourth axis, majors and dependency. Co-responsibility and conciliation refer not only to the care of our children but also to the care of our elders. Ensuring an old age worthy of our elders is one of the main commitments of the Government of Spain. Our elders are the protagonists of our present and our future, and that is why we must continue to work for active ageing and the promotion of healthy lifestyles. Therefore, in this year 2018 the total budget of Imserso amounts to 4 170 million euros, 129 million euros more than in 2017 for the elderly and 500 million euros more in the last five years. Since the Imserso we are promoting, in collaboration with the State Council of Older Persons, the first national strategy for their good treatment and active ageing. In the same vein, the social tourism and thermalism programme will count in 2018 with 105 million euros to continue improving active ageing and to continue contributing to the maintenance of employment in the tourism sector during the low season. With regard to the Unit Care System, we are undertaking a set of measures that are enabling the Unit Act to be preserved and given significant impetus. We are going to allocate a total of EUR 1401 million this year to care for dependency, EUR 46 million more than last year, with the aim that by 2020 we have increased aid for dependency by more than 15% and thus return the effort that older people and families took during the economic crisis. This budget guarantees us a 5% increase in aid to dependency this year. We have also reached a historic agreement between the Government of Spain and the autonomous communities; we have assessed the implementation of the law and have laid the foundations for ensuring attention to today's and future dependence, and we have therefore accredited more than 17,000 professionals, which has made it possible to guarantee employment stability in this sector, and we have incorporated advanced technology into tele-assistance, which will make it possible to provide better service to dependent people. Finally, allow me to highlight the magnificent network of State reference centres in Imserso. In Spain there are seventeen Imserso state reference centres, where innovation, research and the improvement of the quality of resources targeted at people in dependency situations converge. I would like to take advantage of this intervention to announce that these budgets will make it easier for us to open the new State Reference Centre for people with severe neurological disabilities in Langreo, Asturias in a few weeks' time; also, with this budget we will ensure that this year is the reality of the state reference centre in Santiago de Compostela, which is a centre dedicated to physical disability, and also this budget will allow us to continue working to make the state reference centres in León and Soria a reality. The last axis of work of the ministry and of this secretariat of State on which these budgets are designed is the fight against violence against women simply because they are women. These budgets constitute the greatest demonstration of the Government of Spain's unwavering commitment to equality and the fight against violence against women and our children. The Government of Spain and President Rajoy have a firm commitment to the fulfilment and implementation of the State Pact, a commitment that is not initiated last year with the signing of the State Pact, but that whenever it has governed the People's Party has had a firm and unwavering commitment to the fight against gender-based violence, but I do mean that Spain had never before had such a high budget to eradicate and end this scourge in our society. The initial budget presented by the Government in the Courts complies with the budgetary commitment acquired in the state pact because this budget increases by 80 million euros plus the year 2017 to implement the measures of the competition pact of the Government of Spain. If we add to these 80 million increases the already existing allocations in the budget for combating gender-based violence, we have 408 million euros for the measures under the jurisdiction of the Government of Spain. It is the first time in history that there is so much State competition budget in the State General Budgets. The second fulfillment in the initial budget that we present in these Courts is that the State Pact required us to increase the budget of the Government Delegation for Gender Violence, which is dependent on the Ministry, by 20% more, and this Government has increased it by 24% more, reaching almost 40 million euros. I would like to recall that the Socialist Party at the beginning of this parliamentary term demanded that the delegation's budget should reach at least the same level as they had in the 2011 budget: EUR 30 million. For, I repeat, the People's Party government has not only increased what was required by the State Pact by 20%, but has increased it by 24% and we are reaching almost 40 million euros, far from what you had in 2011. [[applause]] And I'll tell you more. This initial budget that we present to the Courts also meets the 120 million for the autonomous communities and municipalities. In this budget that we will approve today, the financing of the autonomous communities is increased by 4234 million euros, providing sufficient resources for the autonomous communities and municipalities to be able to comply with the measures of the pact that are their exclusive competence. This is being done by a People's Party Government because for the People's Party it is a matter of state and, as I have said, an unwavering commitment to women victims, to their children, to their families, to society and to the state pact. Some groups have questioned the autonomous communities, implicitly interpreting that they were not going to comply with the pact with the increase of 4234 million euros. But this Government, as we take it more seriously than others, we have wanted to reach an agreement with the groups by amending the initial budget and agreeing on a compromise amendment that means an increase of EUR 120 million in our budget, the competence of the Government of Spain, to be transferred to the autonomous communities and municipalities to implement the measures of the pact that are within their competence. I also think that today we would have to remember how much money the Government of Rodríguez Zapatero spent in the 2005 budgets for the deployment of the Comprehensive Law, so that autonomous communities and municipalities would deploy the Comprehensive Law. ParlaMint-ES_2018-05-23-CD180523.u35	Madam President, ladies and gentlemen, I am pleased to appear in this House to set out the broad lines of the budget of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs and Cooperation. The consolidated budget for 2018, including the resources allocated to the department, the Spanish Agency for International Development Cooperation and the Cervantes Institute, amounts to EUR 1558 million, with an increase of 3,89 % over the previous year. Specifically, the department's budget for 2018 grows by 1.95% compared to 2017 and stands at 1396.56 million euros. Of this amount, around EUR 214 million corresponds to the non-financial budget and EUR 1182 million to the financial budget. For its part, the budgets of the Aecid and the Cervantes Institute grow in 2018 by 14,6 and 2,83 % respectively. It is an advance that satisfies us, but that does not prevent us, as head of the department, from always aspire to more for our foreign and cooperation policy. With this budget the department faces two fundamental challenges in 2018: our presence as a member of the UN Human Rights Council for the period 2018-2020, after being elected by the General Assembly last October. It is a clear indication of Spain's commitment to human rights, which, as you know, is a priority axis of our foreign policy. The implementation of the V Plan Director of Spanish Cooperation for the period 2018-2021 is the second major challenge. This master plan was presented to this Parliament by the Secretary of State for Cooperation and for Ibero-America and the Caribbean on 20 February and approved by the Council of Ministers on 23 March. The plan transfers to Spanish policy the 2030 Agenda for Sustainable Development in its components of international cooperation. Ladies and gentlemen, after this brief introduction, allow me to structure my presentation around three axes. Firstly, I will comment on the main variations in the department's budget by expenditure chapters; secondly, I will refer to the budgets of Aecid and, thirdly, to those of the Cervantes Institute. As regards the first of its matters, the main changes by expenditure chapters, this draft budget for 2018 provides for staff expenditure EUR 314.74 million, 4% higher than the initial budget for 2017, reaching 26,6% of the non-financial budget of the head department. This includes both the increase in the remuneration of public employees agreed in March between the Administration and the trade unions, and the adjustment to the reality of the planned expenditure in the department, mainly in the cost of the salary equalization module of purchasing power and quality of life of salaries of officials posted abroad. As regards current expenditure on goods and services, its amount amounts to EUR 116.25 million, less than 10% of the department's non-financial budget and 1.07 % above the budgeted expenditure in 2017. This increase is practically located in the strengthening of the security of our representations abroad, quantified at 1.34 million euros. In relation to current and capital transfers, these account for 62.08 % of the non-financial budget of the department and amount as a whole to €733.93 million - 85 % more than in 2017 - of which 287.5 million correspond to transfers made by the department to Aecide and the Cervantes Institute, to which I will refer later. On the rest of the items of expenditure I would like to stop at the following. In the field of the Secretariat of State for Foreign Affairs, mandatory contributions to international agencies and peacekeeping operations reach €426.8 million following increases in 2016 and 2017. This enables us to fulfil our international commitments in time and form and to ensure the reliable country image that is aware of the payment of the contributions. For its part, voluntary contributions to international organizations and entities are provided with €4.1 million, of which €1 million will be allocated to the United Nations High Commissioner for Human Rights. [[Rumors]] Also, among others, there will be contributions to the Permanent Secretariat of the Sahel G5 to enhance gender training, to the fund to cover the participation of Spaniards in election observation missions in the OSCE... [[Rumors]]
ParlaMint-ES_2018-05-23-CD180523.u36	Quiet. Quiet, please.
ParlaMint-ES_2018-05-23-CD180523.u37	... the International Commission against the Death Penalty, the Trust Fund for the Victims of the International Criminal Court, UN Women, the Union for the Mediterranean and the Council of Europe to strengthen their work in the defence and promotion of human rights. In the field of the activities of the Secretary of State for International Cooperation and for Ibero-America and the Caribbean, we will dedicate €9.47 million to the International Financial Facility for Immunization, dedicated to the prevention of infant mortality through the extension of vaccination. We will also allocate €1.15 million for transfers to the International Foundation and to Ibero-America for Administration and Public Policy, to foundations of political parties with parliamentary representation at the national level for cooperation purposes and to non-profit institutions active in the field of development cooperation. ParlaMint-ES_2018-05-23-CD180523.u38	Quiet, please.
ParlaMint-ES_2018-05-23-CD180523.u39	In line with this, we maintain the two nominative grants to associations or horizontal organisations of great European tradition, as well as the competitive grant for the Talk about Europe Programme, which encourages European debate and actions to disseminate European Union policies. On the other hand, the amounts for the protection of Spanish nationals abroad, amounting to EUR 2.35 million, will be allocated to subsistence and extraordinary economic aid, to care institutions and to Spanish nationals deprived of their liberty in foreign prisons, as well as to manage repatriations of Spanish nationals in case of need, with special follow-up in the case of repatriations of victims of gender-based violence. As you know, our consular service is the door to the Spanish Administration abroad and is doing a commendable job in protecting the Spanish abroad. As for the human resources of the MAEC, during 2018, according to the present draft budgets, the ministry will have 6,200 public employees, of which 1504 are destined in the central services and 4696 abroad providing its services in our 118 embassies, 90 consulates and 10 permanent representatives to international agencies. These are limited human resources, given the greater demands that our consular services face, not only because of the increase in the number of Spaniards registered in the Consular Registration, but also because of the increase in tourist movements, as well as the greater presence of Spanish companies abroad. There has also been an increase in visa applications, 40% more between 2006 and 2016, which is a clear indication of our country's attractiveness abroad. Therefore, this department is carrying out a deconcentration process to meet these new needs and sets out as one of its priority objectives the updating of the catalogue of posts abroad, with a view to providing excellent care and service to our citizens abroad. We also seek to maximize the revenues received by the State in terms of consular fees. Specifically, in 2017 around €90 million was raised for the Public Treasury. I continue, ladies and gentlemen, with investments, where we will have EUR 17 million, which will allow us to continue our policy of maintaining and improving our assets abroad, paying particular attention to investments in physical and electronic security of our representations. Finally, in the chapter on financial assets, the allocations of the Water and Sanitation Cooperation Fund and the Development Promotion Fund or Fonprode are maintained at EUR 15 million and EUR 199.23 million respectively. In addition, the Water and Sanitation Cooperation Fund has the possibility of financing new operations through extrabudgetary resources received from completed operations, which are exceptionally included in this exercise in 2017 and 2018. The maximum expenditure ceiling for both concepts is extended by EUR 42 million. In turn, the Fonprode maintains an annual limit of 375 million in terms of the volume of operations that the Council of Ministers may authorise during the financial year from the fund. This cooperation instrument is undergoing a thorough review with the aim of consolidating Spanish financial cooperation and bringing it to levels of funding similar to those of other donors in our environment. Ladies and gentlemen, I now turn to the broad lines of the budget of the Aecid and the Cervantes Institute, as I said before. In 2018, Aecid will manage a budget of 325.96 million euros, 41.5 million more than in 2017. Of this amount, $237.57 million is national funding. The bulk comes from the transfer of the MAEC, which amounts to 221.24 million euros, a 5.34 more than in 2017, i.e. an increase of 11.2 million euros that will be allocated, as a priority, to humanitarian action, non-governmental development organisations and bilateral cooperation actions. In addition, other revenues, such as 1.15 million euros that Aecid will receive from the State Public Employment Service to co-finance the School-Workshop Programme or 15 million euros from other revenues generated by the agency itself. Some EUR 33 million will be added to the abovementioned items as a result of the definitive liquidation of the IRPF for activities of social interest. In addition, Aecid will receive 88.39 million from the European Union for delegated cooperation, compared to 58 million in 2017. Spain is the third managing country of cooperation delegated to the European Union, after Germany and France, which demonstrates the confidence that the Union places in us. That trust is also an important asset of Spanish cooperation. I would like to comment briefly on some of the items of particular relevance to the Aecid expenditure budget. We maintain the allocation of EUR 26 million for the Office for Humanitarian Aid, although our objective is to implement around EUR 36 million at the end of the year, as indicated in the V Plan Director, which says, literally, that in the increase in resources of the Spanish Official Development Assistance, humanitarian aid will be increased to a greater extent in relation to other instruments. The Office for Humanitarian Action, following a strategy applauded by the OECD Development Assistance Committee itself, will focus its efforts on priority countries, inescapable commitments and those acquired at international summits. We will increase funding to development NGOs, which will be carried out through the following instruments. On the one hand, the call for multi-annual agreements, published on 9 April, which will be financed between 2018 and 2021 and will amount to EUR 40 million. On the other hand, three annual calls for development cooperation projects, humanitarian actions and actions in innovation for development. And, thirdly, the nominative subsidy for the coordinator of development NGOs in Spain and the co-financing of the insurance of co-operatives. Ladies and gentlemen, I shall now finish with a reference to the draft budgets of the Cervantes Institute. The Cervantes Institute will count this year with a transfer of the MAEC of 66.2 million euros; a 3.2 more than in 2017. The total expenditure budget of the Cervantes Institute amounts to EUR 3.4 million up to EUR 123.94 million, i.e. it increases by 2.83 %. We will keep the revenues for the activities of the institute for a total amount of 52.4 million euros, once the revenues of the project of nationality started in 2016 have stabilized, after the approval in October 2015 of the new regulations on acquisition of the Spanish nationality. In addition, EUR 500 000 will be allocated to finance the launch of a new headquarters of the Cervantes Institute in Dakar, Senegal, which will be the first centre to be opened in sub-Saharan Africa. Ladies and gentlemen, I would like to have a main idea of this whole presentation. As you can see, the budgets I am presenting today reflect the largest increase in transfers from the State to Spanish cooperation since 2011, since in previous years the situation of inherited excessive deficit had not allowed us to improve its budget allocation. Indeed, once the path of economic growth has begun, it is our intention to work on further increases for the coming fiscal years, because cooperation is a State policy and a priority for our country. We are therefore faced with budgets that recognize the importance of our country's foreign policy and cooperation and the commitment of this Government to both of them is total. As I have had occasion to reiterate at this parliamentary seat, Europe and the international community as a whole require a greater presence of Spain, and we have a duty not to disappoint them. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

We bet, for example, on the arms embargo on Israel and on amendments proposing to increase the departure for the United Nations Agency for Palestine Refugees in the Near East and for multilateral agencies that push the women's rights agenda and support for migrants and asylum seekers; to dignify the working conditions of the external staff of Aecide or to increase scholarships for studies and practices at the Diplomatic School to democratize their access. In addition, the fight against macho violence is central to foreign policy, and here again it is necessary to move from words to facts, because it is regrettable that we have to take away the government from the PP parties in order to comply with something so basic and so cross-cutting. That is why there are three amendments that should be adopted without excuses: one, to provide budgetary support for the 2015 Protocol of Action for the Care of Women Victims of Gender-based Violence Abroad, which has barely been implemented, as the Government itself has recognized us in parliamentary questions and requests for information; one, to fund an equality policy plan in the Ministry itself that addresses the very small number of women in senior positions, which is now only 24%; and another that advocates a cooperation programme to combat gender inequality. In order to remedy the lack of political will, the lack of foreign policy and to ensure transparency, coherence and broad consensus-building, we also consider it necessary for the current Human Rights Office to become an office for policy coherence, human rights and gender equity. We also believe that we need a new external action strategy, drawn up in a participatory manner and consulted with Parliament, the creation of an interterritorial council for foreign policy and action, in order to strengthen coordination between ministries but also between the different territories of the State, and a plan to promote our languages and cultural expressions in Europe and in the European institutions, reflecting the multilingualism, multiculturality and plurinationality of the State. To live up to the challenges of our time, of the geopolitical moment we are living in, we need to match the budget allocated to Casa África, Casa Arab or Casa Sepharad-Israel, as we told them last year; we need to improve the programme for the protection of human rights defenders and we need to increase the allocations for peace processes in the Middle East and Colombia. In cooperation, it is not enough to say again that we will be working to increase the increases in the future, we must increase official development aid in order not to follow the lines of European countries, because at this rate we need fifteen years to reach 0,40, which was the commitment by 2020; we must comply with the Cooperation Act, which says that Aid should be the main agent and, therefore, the funds it manages, as well as the funds managed by NGOs, which are now a residual player, when they should be one of the main players, because they know the sector, the field and the context; we must implement and monitor Agenda 2030 and we must increase monetary aid. This is another commitment that the PP has thrown away. Ten years ago, 350 million were allocated, and this year 19 million. I don't know about you, but I'm just ashamed of this figure. Moreover, since the beginning of the crisis, the number of Spaniards who choose to emigrate has continued to grow. The figure is 2.5 million and, moreover, this emigration is often precarious and highly qualified, without this being exclusive, but it is often left out of consular registration for the health exclusion to which it was condemned under Royal Decree 16/2012. In order to improve their living conditions, we are proposing amendments to strengthen consular assistance services throughout the network to ensure that the requested vote is effective. In the case of the United Kingdom and because of the Brexit, it is urgent to draw up a study on the real dimensions and nature of Spanish emigration. I would like to end with three amendments to Section 34, which, although they do not question the mandatory and already established general contributions to the European Union, do want to show that the heading that Spain is making to the Refugee Fund for Turkey can be used in a completely different way, that it is not compulsory or fixed and that it is currently mostly a militarized management of migratory flows and not in accordance with human rights. The three amendments propose that Spain should be the driving force behind a plan to combat poverty and child exclusion within the European Union, a European strategy for the implementation of the Istanbul Convention on Gender Violence in Migration and Asylum Matters and the implementation of the communication on the protection of unaccompanied migrant children in the European Union. In short, there are thirty-five amendments with which we want to invest more and spend better, putting human rights and people at the centre of foreign policy and cooperation policy, and this is achieved by two things: political will and economic resources. Thank you. [[Applauds]]

I therefore believe that we are taking steps in the right direction. Today, the 112 competent judicial parties of the Ministry have put in place the tools to ensure that the judicial process is fully electronic, as well as the 659 single-person bodies, the 41 sections of the 20 provincial hearings of the Ministry's territory and the 17 chambers of the five higher courts of justice. All of them have the facilities to process, sign, archive and notify using digital tools. That is why we want to continue in this direction, and that is why the budget has EUR 131 million that will be destined to complete the implementation of the digital file in the Supreme Court, as well as in the 39 prosecution offices in the territory of the Ministry of Justice. But we also work with professionals and legal corporations, because we not only work inside but also to serve professionals, lawyers, prosecutors, social graduates, notaries, registrars, creating new services to facilitate their communication with justice. The 131 million are also intended to improve the focus on users, so that they receive better attention when, as is true, incidents arise in the day-to-day justice; to create better tax and digital justice applications; to improve equipment in the rooms of view, for recordings and also for Wi-Fi connections in all of them; or to continue to advance on a project, in which we have worked intensively in this Parliament, such as the new digital civil register. Therefore, ladies and gentlemen, it seems to me that the digital justice bet is part of the thrust of this budget and should be the subject of its approval and support. The consolidated budget for 2018 amounts to €9649.27 million. [[Rumors]]
ParlaMint-ES_2018-05-23-CD180523.u286	Quiet, please.
ParlaMint-ES_2018-05-23-CD180523.u287	This represents an increase of 1.78 % over the 2017 financial year, which was 9480.56, i.e. an increase of 168.71 million euros. Of this amount, the part allocated to the State subsector amounts to EUR 1377 million, while autonomous bodies and the State Meteorology Agency will be allocated EUR 8272 million from the consolidated budget. At the level of the ministry's most prominent spending policies, the 2018 budget will allocate the following amounts: EUR 7463 million for agriculture, fisheries and food policy, 1,3% higher than the 2017 budgets; EUR 1618 million for infrastructure policy, 3.5% higher than the previous year; and EUR 86,3 million for meteorology policy, 2.2% higher than 2017. Ladies and gentlemen, the budgets for 2018 will contribute to the improvement of our agri-food and fisheries sector and to the protection of our environment. I then go on to break down the ministry's broad lines and programmes of action in 2018. [[The Rumors Continue]]
ParlaMint-ES_2018-05-23-CD180523.u288	Silence, ladies and gentlemen. Please, be quiet.
ParlaMint-ES_2018-05-23-CD180523.u289	In relation to Chapter 1, staff costs, the planned allocation for 2018 amounts to EUR 375.6 million, an increase of 2,9 % compared to last year. This appropriation will cover the salaries of the Ministry's staff, which covers 9275 persons. One more year I would like to thank all the Ministry's workers for their efforts in improving the conditions of our agri-food and fisheries sector as well as for their efforts in protecting our environment. Without them this important work would not be possible. With regard to agricultural insurance, the subsidies provided for in the Thirty-ninth Combined Agricultural Insurance Plan amount to EUR 211.27 million, representing an average subsidy of around 30% compared to the total foreseeable cost. This item is expandable and, if necessary, the credit will be increased to ensure that the level of coverage is maintained. Ladies and gentlemen, this government has always relied on agricultural insurance, and that is why we maintain the level of public support and not only through the annual allocations in the State's General Budgets. Year after year, the different insurance schemes have been approved, including measures to promote recruitment by farmers and livestock farmers by improving the different lines and always listening to the demands of the different sectors. Thus, we have introduced measures such as the fractionation of payments to policies supported by SAECA; the subsidy, to the maximum extent permitted by the European Union, of basic insurance lines; or the adoption of concrete solutions to engage farmers and livestock farmers. [[65 %]] All this has allowed the forecast for 2018 to be no better. The insurance levels this year will exceed the guaranteed capital in 2017, which reached 13.7 billion euros, marking a historical record. I think we should be proud of our agricultural insurance system, which is on its way to being forty years old, and from the government we will continue to support it as we have been doing so far. I shall now break down the budget for agriculture, fisheries and food policies, which amounts to 7463 million, 1.3% more than last year, allowing for the continuation of the policies agreed for this parliamentary term. We want the agri-food sector to remain a strategic sector for our economy. In general, we have given priority to actions involving European co-financing in order to maximise the use of Community funds. In 2018 we allocated 104.5 million euros for the financing of rural development programmes of the autonomous communities in which, as you know, the ministry does not participate but does finance. By now entering into the details of the programmes managed by the General Secretariat for Agriculture and Food, for the competitiveness and quality of production and agricultural markets, more than EUR 44 million has been allocated with the aim of increasing agricultural income by enhancing innovation, promoting competitiveness and strengthening the productive structure of farms. The competitiveness and quality of agricultural health programme is worth EUR 35.8 million. The objective of this programme is to ensure compliance with health requirements, traceability and registration of agricultural production, prevention of pests or health and environmental control in the means of production used by producers. This programme consolidates the Ministry's collaboration with the Autonomous Communities, with €6 million for the prevention and control of pests and €8 million for State programmes for the eradication of animal diseases. The competitiveness programme of the agri-food industry and food quality has a budget of EUR 25.3 million to support the agri-food sector, from industrialization, marketing and promotion of final products to the management and vertebration of the agri-food chain. Among our main objectives is the promotion of our agricultural, fisheries and agri-food products, beyond our borders. For this reason, the line of promotion and support for the internationalisation of agricultural, fisheries and agri-food products has increased to EUR 7.1 million, an additional 52%. In this regard, I would like to recall that in 2017 Spanish exports of agri-food products hit a historic record by exceeding EUR 50 billion for the first time. I shall now comment on the three programmes in which the Directorate-General for Rural Development and Forestry Policy is involved and its corresponding budget. The water management programme for irrigation has also increased its budget to €57.7 million. Watering has a fundamental role to play in generating wealth and employment throughout the territory, and we can say that Spanish agriculture is today more competitive thanks to the development of watering and more sustainable thanks to its modernization. The modernization of irrigation avoids the depopulation and ageing of rural areas, one of our main objectives of this legislature. In the same vein and with the aim of creating attractive welfare conditions to set people in rural areas, within the rural development programme, the Directorate-General for Rural Development and Forestry Policy will have EUR 132.1 million. Finally, through the protection and improvement of the natural environment programme, the Directorate-General will allocate EUR 119.3 million to meet the commitments contained in the Forest Action Plan, as the protection of our mountains is of vital importance to us. Under the plan, EUR 82.5 million will be allocated for preventive work and forest fires. To conclude the part of the General Secretariat of Agriculture and Food, I will make a brief mention of two autonomous bodies associated with it: the Agency for Food Information and Control, the AICA, which ensures the proper functioning of the food chain, which this year has a budget of 6.3 million euros; and the Spanish Agricultural Guarantee Fund, which is responsible for the management and control of the European agricultural funds received by Spain, whose budget amounts to 6976 million euros. In short, these budgets of the General Secretariat for Agriculture and Food will allow us to continue to improve the functioning of the agri-food sector and its marketing, to continue to gain new markets and to generate more value and wealth, and, of course, to give stability and competitiveness to our sector in order to ensure its future growth. I turn next to the budget of the Secretariat of State for the Environment, which rises by 3.6% compared to 2017, to 2108,7 million euros. This will enable further progress to be made in meeting the environmental commitments made at both international and national level. In the area of water, the overall budget for this year is over EUR 1688 million, an increase of 4.5% compared to the previous year. Of these, EUR 818 million will be earmarked for investments in water; EUR 536 million for the conservation and maintenance of hydraulic infrastructure; EUR 152 million for the purification of waste water and the improvement of the quality of water bodies and their associated ecosystems. These budgets will enable us to meet environmental objectives, meet demands and improve the management of extreme weather events, both floods and droughts. We will also give continuity to the actions in the area of sanitation and sewage treatment the competence of the State included in the Growth Plan. In the field of costs, the budget allocated is EUR 89.9 million, with 57.7 million of investment through the coastal actions programme. This will contribute to the protection of the marine environment of our coastal systems and to the conservation of the maritime-terrestrial public domain. These budgets also allow progress to be made in implementing the coastal protection strategies already approved and in drafting those that are still pending. The appropriations for the Directorate-General for Quality and Environmental Assessment and the Natural Environment are increased to EUR 28.1 million, 24% more than in 2017. On the one hand, the programme for the protection and improvement of the environment has a budget of EUR 14.2 million. They will be used for the processing of environmental assessment procedures, for the protection of the atmosphere and for the management of environmental risk of chemicals and for progress towards the objectives of the Waste Law and the State Waste Management Framework Plan 2016-2022. In addition, Air Plan II will be developed and we will develop the national air pollution control programme, which will establish measures for reducing emissions. On the other hand, the programme for the protection and improvement of the natural environment has 13.8 million euros for the protection of our biodiversity. The global climate change budget exceeds €55 million, 5% more than in 2017, of which €19.5 million is included in the programme actions for the prevention of pollution and climate change of the Spanish Climate Change Office. In the 2018 budgets, climate change policy is integrated into the programmes of all directorates-general so that the budgets are in the units that implement the measures, thus achieving greater efficiency in their management. The adaptation budget also grows, up to €35.5 million, 4% more than in 2017, and the mitigation budget, which adds up to €17.5 million, 24% more, highlighting the increase in the Carbon Fund reaching €12 million, with an increase of 30,5%. Moreover, the budget of the State Meteorology Agency, Aemet, will amount to EUR 129 million, an increase of 2,1 % over 2017. The budget of the autonomous National Parks agency is 36.2 million euros and the budget for the Biodiversity Foundation, which this year celebrates its twentieth anniversary, amounts to 9.9 million euros, which will be expanded to 24.7 with the European funds it administers to carry out more than fifty initiatives and projects. In short, these budgets of the Secretariat of State for the Environment allow us to continue working to improve the growth model that we want to achieve as a country, as a society and as individuals, since the protection of the environment is a commitment of all and a responsibility not only to the future but also to ourselves. Finally, I am going to break down the budget for fisheries policy, which for this year amounts to more than EUR 56 million. This will enable us to continue our policy in this area, which, as you know, is based on sound scientific knowledge, on strengthening inspection and control and on ensuring the maximum long-term sustainable yield of fisheries resources. To this end, the Secretariat-General for Fisheries has two budgetary programmes: the improvement of fisheries structures and markets programme has EUR 38.8 million and revolves around the following areas. The first is structural policy, with a budget of EUR 13.4 million to meet the needs arising from the implementation of the measures contained in the European Maritime and Fisheries Fund. We will also allocate EUR 6 million to articulate financial instruments that will help boost the competitiveness of the fisheries and aquaculture sector. The second area of activity is the fisheries economy and marketing, to which EUR 7.4 million is allocated, of which 6.5 million is directed to fisheries producer organisations for the financing of their production plans and marketing, overtaking and restructuring and storage mechanism. The third major area of activity is control and inspection, with a budget of EUR 7.8 million. In this regard, I would like to stress that since 2016 our control and inspection system has been a European and international benchmark. It has all the ISO 9001:2015 certification, but we continue to work to maintain and improve it by allocating 4 million euros to the maintenance of the fishing information system, 4.9 for the management of the electronic logbook on board and the Fisheries Monitoring Centre and 8 million for the deployment of means of inspection, in order to ensure compliance with the current regulations. The other programme managed by the General Secretariat for Fisheries is the one for the protection of fisheries resources and sustainable development, which will have 17.3 million euros this year; of them we will allocate 11 million for research and scientific studies, because we believe that having the best scientific advice is essential for the better management of resources and this will help us to move forward in our commitment to fisheries sustainability. Under this programme for the protection of fisheries resources and sustainable development, EUR 4 million will be allocated to marine reserves. We are aware that marine reserves are a long-term investment in biodiversity and sustainable fisheries, and therefore from the government we attach great importance to them as a model of fisheries protection. I'm done, ladies and gentlemen. Through these budgets, the Ministry of Agriculture and Fisheries, Food and Environment will be able to continue to work for the benefit of all Spaniards, especially our farmers, farmers and fishermen, as well as for the improvement and care of our environment. Once again, the People's Party Government is acting in a serious and responsible manner, caring for the needs of citizens. That is why, as I mentioned at the beginning of my speech, we have endeavoured to create the most social budgets in our country's history, budgets designed for people living in rural areas, so that the main economic activity in many of these areas will work, be attractive and generate employment. Because the agri-food sector is strategic for our economy and is shown by the record figures in agricultural income, 27 831 million euros in 2017, 25% more than in 2011, and in the value of the production of the agricultural branch, 49 192 million euros reached in 2017. These are budgets designed to promote social progress and the growth of society, through the care and protection of the environment, with policies aimed at improving the quality of life of people in terms of air quality or the protection of the coast and the natural environment, among others. In short, ladies and gentlemen, these budgets will allow Spain to continue along the path of balanced and sustainable growth and stable job creation, improving the lives of all people. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]
