Thus, the international situation is characterized by a strong and widespread economic expansion, with a global GDP that is accelerating to 3.9 % this year, largely thanks to the good behaviour of international trade. On the other hand, the projected growth in the euro area, of particular importance for Spain, is 2.4%, one tenth higher than in 2017. However, the Government has reiterated that there are downside risks stemming primarily from protectionist threats from major trading partners, from high levels of indebtedness at the global and also national levels, although the forecast is that short-term rates will remain stable in negative terrain for some quarters. Also, as is well known, geopolitical tensions in the Middle East are pushing up the price of oil and can pose a risk to our economy. However, as far as the Spanish economic situation is concerned, three fundamental facts stand out. Firstly, the Spanish economy has been growing at a rate of more than 3% for three years, ahead of all the major economies of the European Union. This growth, in the case of Spain, is being translated in a very remarkable and historically unprecedented way into a great creation of jobs. As a result, the unemployment rate has been reduced to a historically rapid speed of more than 10 points in five years. This good behavior, ladies and gentlemen, will also be maintained during 2018. In fact, as you know, the government has revised GDP growth projected for this year by four tenths to 2.7%. This upward revision is based primarily on two positive developments; firstly, the improvement of the external environment and fundamentally the euro area; and secondly, the dynamism of gross capital formation, i.e. business investment, which has grown and is growing in a very strong tone in recent months. Our growth forecast, ladies and gentlemen, is credible, is in line with that of the main international bodies and I would just like to mention the International Monetary Fund, which gives us 2.8%, that is, one tenth above our own forecast; the Bank of Spain, which gives us 2.7%; and recently the European Commission itself, which gives us 2.9%, or two tenths above our own forecast. As you know, two weeks ago the Commission rose to 2.9%, more than half a point above our forecast for growth in relation to the last forecast, which was from the previous autumn. Let me stress that this economic expansion that is already entering its fourth year is taking place in a context of price stability, with moderate inflation and based on two engines that are operating simultaneously, which is a historic first for the Spanish economy. On the one hand, the external demand that, as I said, is growing very positively and adding to the growth of the economy. It is the first time in our recent history that both demands, external and internal, are contributing positively to the growth of the Spanish economy. This will allow us to keep the current account balance at a positive figure of around 1.7%. In this way, Spain will chain - something unprecedented in our economy - seven years of surplus from our current accounts and our external balance. This shows that today our economy is more competitive and shows that our growth pattern is healthier and above all more sustainable; that is, it is the best guarantee that the economic growth we are seeing will not be strangled in the future, as was usual in the past, but that the Spanish economy is in a position to continue to grow. In contrast to what happened a few years ago, sustainable growth is not accompanied by over-appropriation of financial resources from abroad or by the indebtedness of the Spanish economy vis-à-vis the rest of the world. On the contrary, we are systematically reducing our external indebtedness and maintain, as I have already pointed out, a comfortable external financing situation. On the other hand, the private sector is making a significant effort to decoupling, with a 60-point reduction in public sector debt in relation to gross domestic product. All this means, ladies and gentlemen, that the Spanish economy has changed. We have a different economy than we had in 2011. With the government presided over by Mariano Rajoy, the Spanish economy is different, the Spanish economy has changed. This remarkable performance is also noted in the labour market. Between 2014 and 2017, 2 million jobs have been created and the growth threshold, from which the Spanish economy begins to create jobs, has been significantly reduced, taking into account our recent economic history. The forecast for this year 2018 is that, in terms of the labour force survey, 475 000 new jobs will be created, thus increasing the annual average, in an economy that is going to grow to 2.7%, will be 2.5%. These data confirm that we are on the right track to meet the Government's commitment to reach 20 million occupied by the beginning of 2020. Obviously, these good forecasts - and I will never tire of repeating it - cannot ignore that we still have a lot of work to do and that we have to persevere in job creation. Furthermore, we are aware that there are these challenges that I mentioned, that not everything is done and that the biggest mistake we could make right now is to deviate from this economic policy that is giving us results, especially in terms of employment for all Spaniards. This effort is also accompanied by a very clear commitment from the Government to fiscal discipline and the reduction of our public accounts. The draft budget foresees that the Spanish economy will abandon the so-called corrective arm of the Stability and Growth Pact, which has just been ratified by the European Commission this morning, having declared that the budget is in compliance with European standards this year. I turn now, ladies and gentlemen, to detail the budget for Section 27, which corresponds to the Ministry of Economy, Industry and Competitiveness, which this year, as you have seen, will grow by 3.39% to almost 8 billion, 7933 million euros. It is a figure that exceeds EUR 13.7 billion if the allocations for the eleven agencies that are attached to this ministry are also taken into account. Of these nearly EUR 8 billion, slightly more than EUR 3 billion corresponds to non-financial chapters and the remaining EUR 4 900 are financial envelopes. The main changes with respect to last year's accounts are: firstly, an increase in Chapter 1 to meet the wage increase commitments of public employees; secondly, a reduction of almost EUR 30 million in the negative remuneration of Treasury funds and, thirdly - and let me highlight this - an increase of EUR 91.6 million, of almost EUR 92 million, of non-financial expenditure for the State Secretariat of R & D+i, without forgetting, of course, an increase of EUR 195 million in contributions to public sector enterprises for the development of technological and industrial projects linked to defence programmes and of EUR 150 million for Hisdesat to develop a new communications satellite. Ladies and gentlemen, I will now focus on the appropriations for the various departments of the ministry. First, the State Secretariat of Economy and Enterprise Support will have almost €670 million. This amount is reduced with respect to 2017 mainly because of the lower contributions to international financial institutions, since, for calendar reasons, we do not have to deal with the quotas of this type of multilateral agency of which Spain is a partner. Moreover, as you know, this department is responsible for the State's financing strategy through the Treasury General Secretariat. In this sense, the forecast for 2018 is to emit, in gross terms, slightly more than EUR 215.3 billion, almost 8% less than last year. This includes a maximum of EUR 15 164 million which will be used to finance social security through a loan of EUR 13 830 million and a transfer for the remaining amount. As a result of the fall in Treasury financing costs and lower government financing needs, since 2014 the ratio of financial expenditure to GDP has been on a steadily declining path. This will undoubtedly help the recent improvement in the ratings of the four major international rating agencies. Thus, for this year, the heading will amount to slightly more than €31.5 billion, below, in terms of GDP, than observed for 2017. This lower cost of reducing debt servicing is due to the considerable reduction that the risk premium has experienced in recent years, which benefits not only State financing, as mentioned above, but the entire economy; it benefits businesses, banks and, ultimately, it benefits mainly small and medium-sized enterprises, which, as you know, are currently financed in Spain under better conditions, even in the central countries of the euro area. With regard to the Treasury's financing strategy, it will continue to be similar to that of recent years, based on extending the average life of debt, reducing the average financing costs and advancing the diversification of the investment base, for which we have the undoubted help of improving the qualifications to which I referred earlier. For this reason, we estimate that the public debt-to-GDP ratio will be reduced this year, according to a gradual and constantly downward path, to 97.5% at the end of the year, almost 1.5 points below the end of the next financial year. And in the Stability Programme, as you know, we expect in 2021 to fall below 90% in the debt-to-GDP ratio. As for the Secretariat of State for Trade, it will have EUR 425.4 million, which will mainly be used to consolidate the evolution of the Spanish foreign sector and to support the Spanish company in its internationalisation. In fact, this area takes 98 % of the state secretariat's allocation, EUR 416 million, which will serve to finance two main headings for our foreign trade: the biennial plan for the internationalization of the Spanish economy 2017-2018 and the instruments for financial support to the Spanish company. With regard to the biennial plan, its priorities for the 2018 financial year are: to increase the size of the exporting company, to design a promotional programme in target countries to strengthen our position as a foreign investment destination, to prioritise the innovation and digitisation of the exporting company, and to diversify our destination markets, paying particular attention to Asia and in particular to key markets such as China, Japan or India. As for financial support instruments for companies, they remain at the same levels as in 2017, ensuring that they can fully meet their commitments to finance internationalisation. Thus, the FIEM, Fund for the Internationalization of the Enterprise, is endowed with 218 million and a maximum approval ceiling of 500 million. FIEX, the Fund for Foreign Investments, and the Fund for Foreign Investment Operations of the SME, Fonpyme, are endowed with 12 million and a ceiling of approval, respectively, of 300 million and 35 million. On the other hand, a key element for the support to the exporting company, such as export credit insurance, managed by Cesce, will have an approval ceiling of EUR 9 billion, which we estimate perfectly in a position to support the Spanish exporting sector, which has been recording such good behaviour lately. ICEX, for its part, also sees its endowment increased to 80.5 million, which will allow to increase activities to promote the internationalization of the Spanish company. With regard to internal trade, the Secretariat of State will have almost twice last year's budget, with a total of 9 million, to develop the new plan to support the competitiveness of retail trade, which is currently being discussed and approved at the relevant sectoral conference. This plan, ladies and gentlemen, particularly wants to influence the digitisation of small trade in order to promote export by retail trade for Spanish tourists who want to continue to have Spanish products when they return to their places of origin. I now turn to the budget of the State Secretariat for R & D & I, which is, as you know, the one that absorbs most of our ministry's budget. Specifically, this area will receive €4,612 million, 5.2% more than in 2017, which is fully reflected in non-financial allocations. Therefore, ladies and gentlemen, there is a very particular effort by the Government and this year's budget to support investment in R & D+i. This endowment increase and this particular effort in R+D+i will allow the Secretary of State to guarantee all the calls of the state plan in better conditions, to launch new calls such as so-called proofs of concept and, ultimately, to favor the presence of our country in the major international research infrastructures. With regard to IPOs, the public research bodies will also benefit from an increase of EUR 10 million in their budget allocation, which will mainly be used to improve and materialise the wage increases agreed with the public service unions. Ladies and gentlemen, the new State Research Agency is improving its allocation by EUR 30 million to reach EUR 640 million, while the CDTI will receive an additional EUR 20 million to credit and finance the centres of the so-called Cervera Network, one of the main developments in this year's budget. Of the EUR 480 million in Chapter 8, EUR 80 million will be allocated to a guarantee fund to facilitate the application for guarantees by all companies wishing to join. Finally, let me remind you that, beyond the figures, the draft budgets also contain very positive measures for the Spanish science system - and this is an issue on which I would like to make a particular impact. In particular, a job stabilization rate for research staff is included, IPOs are allowed to carry out the pay increases provided for in the Science Act and are exempted from prior intervention - and this is a particularly relevant issue - to projects financed in whole or in part from European funds, which is one of the best news for the Spanish science system for the coming years, especially in view of the expected large increase in the allocations of the new Horizon Europe Plan. As far as the Secretary-General for Industry and SMEs is concerned, the draft budget for 2018 covers an amount of EUR 2146 million, 22.6% more than last year. The bulk of this heading is intended to provide financial support to industry and, above all, to SMEs. In this respect, the key programmes will be: the Reindus programmes, reindustrialisation and strengthening of industrial competitiveness, with EUR 811 million, for investments in improving competitiveness; the digital transformation actions, a key element of any industrial policy today, which will have EUR 100 million; the financing lines for strategic projects in the development of priority sectors, with EUR 135 million; and, finally, subsidies for the acquisition of vehicles powered by alternative energy, which will have EUR 16.6 million. Finally, there are provisions to subsidise interest compensation to modernize the naval sector, with 57 million; to carry out technological projects in the defence sector, with 467 million euros; or to keep Spain at the forefront of European policies in the space sector, with 360 million euros. With regard to the SME sector, a central element of the Spanish economy and of job creation, support for the entrepreneurial initiative is maintained; in particular, through Enisa, which will be able to mobilise almost EUR 99 million in participatory loans, and from the Spanish Reaffirmation Company, Cersa, which will be able to grant guarantees to more than EUR 12 000 enterprises worth EUR 26 million. In addition, the Secretary General of Industry will approve the strategic industrial framework for Spain in the coming years and is also finalizing a specific strategic plan for SMEs. Ladies and gentlemen, I'm done. The adoption of this budget, which includes such a favourable macro-framework for the Spanish economy, is essential for many reasons. It is essential to improve the calls for innovation and research, to implement job improvements for civil servants or to be able to grant aid to thousands of companies, in particular thousands of small and medium-sized enterprises. But it is not just that; the adoption of these general budgets is essential to ensure the political and economic stability of our country and to strengthen the image and confidence in the credibility of Spanish economic policy, which has just been endorsed by the European Commission's opinion this morning eloquently, which I would like to thank from this House. This in itself is a value for the country, contributes to continuing to drive our economy and, above all, contributes to the continuation of the process we are seeing of job creation, which is the best response to the social challenges our country has. That is why I call on all groups, also opposition groups that have not yet joined the Government, to support with their vote these good budgets for the Spanish economy. Thank you very much.
